# Roadmap

The full-product build order — **the milestone spine**, risk-first, each milestone a runnable slice that *proves* one thing. This is sequencing, not design: each entry names what it proves and the [VISION](../VISION.md) workflow(s) it lands; the milestone-level *what* and *why* live in the design docs. For scope and the determinism boundary see [CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md); for the *why* of the ordering, [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md).

This doc owns the **milestone spine** plus the increment decomposition of milestones *already planned*. A milestone is turned into ordered increments by the [milestone-planning workflow](milestone-planning-workflow.md) **when it is picked up** — never all milestones up front — so M2–M8 below stay milestone-only until then, while M1 (shipped) carries its full decomposition. Work-unit terms are `milestone > increment > task` ([structural-grammar.md](../design/structural-grammar.md) → Work-units); **tasks are not enumerated** — they're cut per-increment at pickup, so the list stays honest against what prior increments produced. *How* an increment goes from "not started" to "validated and committed" is the [increment workflow](increment-workflow.md); how a milestone *opens* and *closes* are the [planning](milestone-planning-workflow.md) and [completion](milestone-completion-workflow.md) workflows.

The ordering principle is **risk-first**: stand up the spine, retire the #1 technical risk in isolation, build the loop, prove the differentiators, then widen. The spine is strictly linear — each milestone builds on the one before.

## The milestone spine

Each entry is milestone-level only — its increments are cut by the [milestone-planning workflow](milestone-planning-workflow.md) when the milestone is picked up. M1 is shipped; its decomposition follows below.

### M1 · single-task execution loop — ✅ shipped (2026-05-31)
The core loop (discover → compose → execute → validate → finalize), proving it beats a plain `CLAUDE.md` via the superseding-decision flow. VISION: `single task execution`. **Decomposition below.**

### M2 · router + second work-workflow — ✅ shipped (2026-06-01)
A second work-workflow (`quick-fix` — commit-only, `allows-create: []`) ships and the cascade's `default-workflow` flips from `single-task` to a **router** (`creates-task: false`), so `jigc start "<intent>"` routes to a model-free selection among the work-workflows. **Proves:** the orthogonal `creates-task` / `default-workflow` knobs (designed in [write-commands.md](../design/write-commands.md), unexercised in M1) hold, and the model-free selection path. No new doctype. *Engine cost (corrected from the first-draft "no engine change" after the planning review — see [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md)):* a **minimal engine-native `catalog` data-value root** exposing the live work-workflow list to composition, plus the **`creates-task: false` compose contract** (no task minted, intent threaded by agent-substitution). Form D (`jigc start --workflow <X>`) was designed but never built in M1.

### M3 · spec-driven planning — ✅ shipped (2026-06-02)
The `spec` doctype + the flow that creates it, so a spec-driven workflow reads `{{@task.spec#…}}` (the MVP was deliberately spec-less). **Decomposition below.** **Proves:** the doc-creation differentiator beyond `adr`, and the intent → spec → implementation arc — `spec` earns its schema from its real creator and consumer, not up front. VISION: `project planning` / `milestone planning`.

### M4 · override application
Recorded **deltas** — `scalar-set` · `structural-op` by id (insert/replace/remove) · `slot-fill` · `tracked-fork` — applied through the cascade's 9-phase resolution so a project composes a workflow/schema **differently from the pack default without forking** ([overrides.md](../design/overrides.md)). **Proves:** principle #5's *individualize* half — customize one thing against a versioned base via tracked deltas, the cascade wired **live** (today the resolver is a unit-proven pure function with no runtime feeder — `cascade::resolve` is called only for the provenance header; compose reads from a single layer). **Sequenced before fan-out** because customization is core to the adoption story and touches far more of the system than the bounded concurrency primitive does.

### M5 · upgrade reconciliation
The other half of principle #5: the engine-native **`override-default` probe** + a guarded **`jigc upgrade`** that re-applies each recorded delta against a new pack version and reports **clean / conflict / orphaned** (stateless `base-hash` compare). On `conflict` it **blocks with a review route** (keep / re-target / drop, showing what changed) — the true 3-way-merge authoring is deferred (matching `file_state`'s own deferral, [reconciliation.md](../design/reconciliation.md)); `override-default`'s checks ship **blocking-by-default** (cascade-tunability is M6). **Proves:** principle #5's *inherit-upstream* half — *inherit every upstream improvement for free; every divergence surfaces at a known moment* — entirely unbuilt in code today (no `upgrade` command, no probe, no version trigger). Rides on M4's recorded-delta substrate — hence immediately after it ([overrides.md](../design/overrides.md) → Upgrade reconciliation). *(Split from the old combined M5 — see [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) 2026-06-03; the severity subsystem the probe's tunability rests on became M6.)*

### M6 · severity tuning & demotion-lock
The validation-severity subsystem the cascade has always promised but no probe yet honors: **severity assigned via the cascade** (every tunable probe site reads `validation.<probe>.<check>.severity` from `project > team > pack-default` instead of a hardcoded literal), **per-check knob granularity** (replacing M4's per-probe keys), the **intrinsic / tunable two-tier** model with the **demotion-lock floor** (a `scalar-set` demoting an intrinsic check below `blocking` is rejected at cascade resolution), and a **non-task probe entry point** so non-task-scoped probes (like `override-default`) participate. **Proves:** "severity is a cascade setting, never a code change" ([validation.md](../design/validation.md) → Severity inventory) holds end-to-end. **Sequenced here** to finish the override/validation theme (M4 apply · M5 reconcile · M6 tune) before the concurrency primitive — and because nothing in the product yet *needs* tunable severities, so it follows the milestone that introduces the first tunable probe (`override-default`). *(Split from the old combined M5 — see [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) 2026-06-03.)*

### M7 · milestone execution — the deterministic join core (data plane)
The engine half of the single bounded concurrency primitive: a **`milestone` work-unit** (`milestone > task`, one shared base) with task-list verbs (`create` / `add-task` / `add-from-spec`), **isolated sub-task area dirs** + a **join-time isolation check**, **parallel doc minting** with a deterministic task-id-ordered collision-suffix + intra-doc self-ref rewrite (over `created` vs `edited-from-base` provenance), and the **by-task-id join merge** (edge-index "site 4") — a disjoint union, **blocking** on a same-doc clash, with **cross-area refs rejected** (resolved per sub-area against `committed ∪ that sub-task's own area` only). **Proves:** the join is a *pure function of the set of sub-task areas* — reproducible regardless of completion order — proven by **permutation tests over real multi-area fixtures** (not a real agent spawn; that's M8). The **highest architectural risk**, isolated to the engine data plane. *(Split from the old combined M7 — see [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) 2026-06-04; the spawn binding + workflow + genuine end-to-end became M8.)* VISION: `milestone execution`.

### M8 · milestone execution — spawn binding + acceptance (control plane) — ✅ shipped (2026-06-05)
The assistant half: **`fan-out`/`join` step kinds** + the spawn-instruction emit class, the **`{{milestone.tasks}}` data-value root** the fan-out step consumes, the **adapter spawn binding** + the `jigc workflow W --task <id>` sub-agent re-entry + the **write-time `--task`-scoped barrier**, the **`milestone-execution` workflow** that composes the fan-out over `{{milestone.tasks}}` (entered via `jigc milestone execute <id>`), and the **genuine real-Task-tool end-to-end** acceptance ([worked-examples.md](../design/worked-examples.md) flow 10, driven by real spawn). **Proves:** parallel sub-agent work stays reproducible *under the blackboard model end-to-end* — the CLI emits spawn instructions deterministically, the adapter launches, the M7 join recombines. Rides directly on M7's data plane. *(Split from the old combined M7 — see [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) 2026-06-04.)* VISION: `milestone execution`. **Decomposition below.**

### M9 · project setup (new & existing) — ✅ shipped (2026-06-06)
The setup workflows: bootstrap a **new** project (with idea development) and **ingest an existing** one. **Proves:** jigc wires into a real project from zero. **Last of the foundational spine** because it is the broadest and least-defined — legacy ingestion was flagged research-grade in [VISION.md](../VISION.md) → Open questions (bounded to detect-and-route; auto-migrate deferred). VISION: `project setup (existing)` + `project setup (new + idea development)`. **Decomposition below.**

## The post-spine arc (M10–M14)

The foundational spine (M1–M9) is complete — the MVP loop, the override/validation theme, the milestone-execution primitive, and project setup all shipped. The post-spine arc completes the product: the **last deferred differentiator** (doc↔code validation), the **introspection surface**, the **designed terminus** (self-hosting), the **doctype/surface completion** the terminus reveals, and the **generalization** (multi-pack). Created 2026-06-06, shaped suggestion-by-suggestion ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) 2026-06-06). Still strictly linear and milestone-level only — each is decomposed by the [milestone-planning workflow](milestone-planning-workflow.md) when picked up. The one hard dependency: **multi-pack (M14) needs a second pack, which self-hosting (M12) mints** — so it sits last.

### M10 · doc↔code validation — the pack-provided probe family
The first pack-provided **probe** and first pack-provided **field type** — the embedded dev pack already supplies doctypes/workflows/steps/commands, so what's new here are the *probe* and *field-type* extension axes (engine-native until now): the **subprocess probe contract** + the **pack-probe determinism contract** ([validation.md](../design/validation.md) — six rules + four meta-finding failure modes) that let the *dev pack* supply `doc↔code` checks, plus the **`code-anchor`** field type (+ adjudicator) that lets `adr`/`spec` reference code structurally. **Proves:** VISION principle #6's headline — *validate against reality, the integration advantage* — documentation drift caught deterministically at the task boundary (an `adr` citing code that no longer exists; a `spec` criterion that maps to no test), the differentiator deferred since the MVP shipped only the engine-native probes (`workflow-refs`, `file-state`). **Bounded slice (to decompose at pickup):** the mechanism + **symbol/file existence** (floor) + **criterion→test mapping** (headline — the VISION example + the spec's reason to exist) + the `code-anchor` field type; **staleness** (code changed after the doc) a stretch. **The determinism contract is *satisfied, not waived*** — the six rules (no network/model/time/random, read-only fs outside scratch, bounded resources, JSON-only IO) are binding and the meta-findings detectable without OS isolation (timeout / crash / malformed-output) are enforced ([validation.md](../design/validation.md) → Pack-probe determinism contract: *"cannot ship without satisfying the contract"*). What's **deferred** is **OS-level sandboxing** (seccomp / Landlock) + the sandbox-violation meta-finding it backs, under the **trusted-pack** model (the only pack is our own; safely running an *untrusted* third-party probe is the post-MVP "not a public API yet" line). Whether `doc-code` ships subprocess-from-the-start or as an in-process prototype that still satisfies the contract is an M10-planning call. Targets the shipped `adr`/`spec`; `arch-doc↔code` rides the same mechanism at M13. VISION: the validate loop (`jigc validate`).

### M11 · describe / introspection — the self-description surface
`jigc describe` — an on-demand **prose projection of the resolved definitions** (`project > team > pack-default`) an LLM calls (or is pointed at via left-behind pointers) to orient on *what can be composed here and how it's used*: the **menu**, distinct from bootstrap's runtime-nudge, `--explain`'s single-command, and validation's correctness-check ([design/introspection.md](../design/introspection.md) — direction locked 2026-05-30, planned + promoted 2026-06-07). **Proves:** the document model applied to jigc describing *itself* — structure CLI-owned, prose human-authored, assembly deterministic and cascade-reflecting; it can't drift because it's generated from the same definitions that drive composition. **Two constraints (locked):** *facts, not advice* (what exists + how it's used, never "you should compose X" — advisory output would hand structural judgment back to the LLM, the thesis inverted one level up); and *non-contractual by design, enforced by format* — the authored `description:`/`usage:` prose is *assembled* through the cascade (the CLI makes no LLM calls) and stays discursive/**hostile to parsing** so nothing depends on it (dissolving the "not a public API" non-goal). **The substantive design work (the open thread, to settle at planning):** which definition types carry the authored fields — **workflows + doctypes the floor**, command-refs + data-value roots a stretch — required-vs-optional per type, and the **cascade merge semantics** (replace vs append when a project overrides a pack's authored prose). De-parked at M11 planning: promoted to [design/introspection.md](../design/introspection.md), wired into the reading order. **Ordering:** before self-hosting (M12) deliberately — the methodology-pack dogfood is exactly what an agent would `describe` to learn the methodology. *Caveat (for M11 planning):* `describe` projects **usage/intent prose** (`description:`/`usage:`), **dialect-shape-agnostic** by the facts-not-mechanism constraint, so it survives M12's dialect extensions; if M11 planning finds the projection must reflect step *structure*, re-order M11 after M12. VISION: a new read-path surface (the introspection loop).

### M12 · self-hosting — distill the harness into a methodology pack + dogfood *(exploratory)*
The project's **designed terminus** ([self-hosting.md](../design/self-hosting.md)) — and deliberately **exploratory**, not a locked design. Three moves: **distill** the hand-run harness (the [planning](milestone-planning-workflow.md) / [increment](increment-workflow.md) / [dev](dev-workflow.md) / [completion](milestone-completion-workflow.md) workflows + their folded learnings) into portable, project-agnostic form (the four-part graduation pass — portability · rationale index · enforced-vs-trusted · a forward-review of the process itself); **encode** it as a jigc *methodology pack* (workflows as definitions, gates as probes/steps); **dogfood + measure** on a fresh project, tracking halts/fix-rounds/audit-findings against the M1–M9 hand-run baseline. **Proves:** jigc can run its own development methodology on a fresh codebase — and, as a forcing function, **sorts the harness into the three-way determinism cut** the product exists to draw: *mechanizable step* (becomes a probe/lint) · *irreducible judgment* (stays the agent's — gap-detection, design-review, settle) · *owner-assigned recorded artifact* (real but un-automatable — the M8 genuine-spawn class). **Honest caveat:** distilling-into-jigc ≠ automating judgment — jigc owns the *structure* (compose, place writes, walk deterministic gates), the agent fills the judgment slots. **The hidden weight (why exploratory):** encoding multi-phase, human-gated, fan-out workflows as jigc definitions needs **workflow-as-managed-artifact dialect surface beyond the MVP** (human-gate steps, conditional phases, bounded fix-loops, the owner-artifact gate) — which **can only be designed by attempting the encode**. So M12's first slice is *distill + encode one workflow (the dev-workflow is simplest) + a bounded dogfood*, and the attempt **may reveal a dedicated workflow-dialect-extension milestone** (named when M12 surfaces its shape — not guessed now). **What M12 dogfoods (the M13 seam):** M12 encodes the *workflows*; the methodology's own working docs (roadmap / deferral-ledger / decisions-log) stay **plain markdown** — **direct/unmanaged file edits under workflow instruction, not jigc-placed writes** (the "jigc places every write" proof, [self-hosting.md](../design/self-hosting.md), waits for M13's doctypes) — the encoded workflows read/write them at fixed paths; *graduating* them to managed doctypes is the M13 fold-back (a fold-back can't precede the thing it enables). Read this consequence honestly: M12's baseline comparison measures **workflow-composition fidelity**, not yet self-managed methodology docs. (If M12 planning finds the dogfood can't be faithful without a specific doctype — e.g. `Scope` mechanically consuming a *managed* deferral-ledger — promote that one doctype into M12, leaving the rest in M13.) **Done/good:** a fresh project, jigc-driven, **matches or beats the hand-run build-health trend** on a comparable-difficulty milestone *and* the judgment slots stay genuinely exercised (not hollowed). Open thread: which project to dogfood — ideally **unlike jigc**, so jigc-shaped gates aren't flattered by a jigc-shaped project. **Prereqs:** M7/M8/M9 ✅ for the *substrate*; the **workflow-as-managed-artifact dialect surface** that [self-hosting.md](../design/self-hosting.md) + [VISION.md](../VISION.md) name as a hard prerequisite is deliberately **re-cast here as *discovered by attempting the encode*** (the hidden-weight note above), not pre-built — so "substrate prereqs ✅" is not "fully unblocked." **What M12 composes (the M14 seam):** M12's dogfood runs the **methodology pack as the sole composed pack** — whatever dev surfaces it needs (`commit`/`adr`, the dev loop) are **vendored into or subsumed by** the methodology pack, *not* co-composed with a separate dev pack; **true dev + methodology multi-pack co-composition is M14's distinct proof**. This breaks the M12↔M14 circularity (M14 needs the pack M12 mints, so M12 must not need M14's composition to run). Whether the methodology pack *subsumes* the dev surfaces or stays thin (so only M14 composes it with dev) is an M12-planning fork. **Mints the second (methodology) pack M14 needs.** *Planning settled 2026-06-07 — de-parked to [self-hosting.md](../design/self-hosting.md); the four forks resolved (reduced-linear dev-workflow encode · subsume · composition-fidelity bar · `gherrink-galey` dogfood) and the decomposition cut below.*

### M13 · doctype & surface completion — the self-hosting fold-back
Acts on what M12's dogfood reveals: the doctypes the methodology needs that don't exist yet, each **earning its schema from its driving workflow** (the M3-`spec` / M9-`prd` discipline), plus the fold-back of M12's emergent learnings. **Two halves.** (1) **`arch-doc` finally earns its driver** — *living architecture documentation*, named in VISION's starting set and driverless since ([doctype-map.md](doctype-map.md): the `cites→adr` edge already drafted), via a general architecture-documentation workflow; this also activates **`arch-doc↔code`** over M10's probe mechanism (the richest doc↔code case, now with a doc to check). (2) **The methodology's own working docs graduate from hand-run markdown to managed doctypes** — the **roadmap**, the **deferral-ledger** ([decisions-pending.md](decisions-pending.md)), the **decisions-log** ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md)) — each with the authoring workflow that creates/maintains it. **Proves:** VISION's named *starting set* is complete (`arch-doc` was its last unbuilt doctype), **plus** the methodology working-doc candidates (roadmap / deferral-ledger / decisions-log) folded back as managed doctypes *if M12 earns them* — so jigc manages the methodology's own documents, the self-hosting loop closed. **Two honesty flags:** the exact doctype set is **candidate, not locked** — it's confirmed at planning *after* M12 surfaces what the dogfood actually needs (the four above are the visible candidates); and each is an **engine/pack-split call** (a roadmap / deferral-ledger is broadly useful → dev pack; a decisions-log may be methodology-pack-specific). Depends on M12 (folds back what it revealed); before M14 (richer doctypes only help multi-pack). *Surface completion = the named graph, **nodes and edges**:* M13 planning also triages the still-open named relations (e.g. `spec —decided-by→ adr`, [doctype-map.md](doctype-map.md)) in-or-out, not just the `arch-doc` node. VISION: completes the `arch-doc` row in [doctype-map.md](doctype-map.md). **Planning settled 2026-06-07 — M13 = half #1 (`arch-doc`) only; half #2 (methodology working-docs → managed doctypes) deferred behind the dialect-extension milestone** (its drivers, the planning/completion workflows, are unencoded — M12 earned zero methodology doctypes), per [architecture-documentation.md](../design/architecture-documentation.md) + [decisions-pending.md](decisions-pending.md). The human chose the **repeatable-component** arch-doc shape (real engine surface: a per-field-type `check:` predicate selector + the item-authoring verbs), relations triage → only `cites → adr` IN. **Decomposition below.**

### M14 · multi-pack composition
One project composing **>1 domain pack** at once (dev + methodology) — how packs compose in the cascade. Today the cascade is `project > team > pack-default` with a **single** pack-default; M14 makes pack-default a composition of **multiple** packs, with deterministic **collision resolution** (two packs defining the same doctype / workflow / command id) and pack precedence/namespacing. **Proves:** the architectural invariant *engine-neutral, packs supply content* generalizes — real architecture validated at scale, not just internal discipline. **Bounded slice (at planning):** compose **our two packs only** (dev + methodology) with deterministic collision rules. The bound is at the **publication** boundary, not the *generality* one: the precedence/namespacing **mechanism is inherently general** (it can't honestly be cut to a two-pack special case), but it **stays internal — not published or stabilized as a public pack-authoring API**. VISION's *"not a second domain / not a public extension API"* non-goal holds at that publication line (two packs compose internally without jigc becoming a public pack platform). **Blocked until a real second pack exists — M12 mints it (the methodology pack), so this sits last; and M12's dogfood deferred dev + methodology co-composition to here, so M14 is its distinct proof, not a repeat.** VISION: [Open questions](../VISION.md) → Multi-pack composition.

## The adoption arc (M15–M17)

The post-spine arc (M10–M14) completed the *product*; this arc **completes self-hosting and then proves adoption in the wild**. M12 ran only a *reduced-linear* dogfood (the dev-workflow, judgment steps as prose); M15 + M16 **close the self-hosting loop** (encode the *harder* harness workflows + graduate the methodology's own working docs to managed doctypes), and M17 runs jigc on *real* projects with a *measure* of whether it actually helps the agent. Created 2026-06-08 after the post-spine arc closed, on the human's direction (multi-domain composition proven → drop the single-domain framing; build the two self-hosting-completion prerequisites, then dogfood-and-measure). Milestone-level only — each decomposed by the [milestone-planning workflow](milestone-planning-workflow.md) at pickup. *Settled at pickup:* **M15** shipped the increment-workflow encode (the `checkpoint` step kind, 2026-06-09); the **M15/M16 boundary** resolved by moving the deferred planning/completion encodes into **M16**, which now **fuses** them with the working-doc → doctype graduation (earn-from-driver). **Gap-filling is need-driven** ([decisions-pending.md](decisions-pending.md)): M15 + M16 are the *only* deferrals built **ahead** of need (the human-set prerequisites); every other deferral fills **when a dogfood surfaces it as actually blocking**, never speculatively.

### M15 · workflow-dialect extension — ✅ shipped (2026-06-09)
Encoding the harness's **harder** workflows (increment / planning / completion) as jigc definitions needs dialect surface the MVP lacks — M12's reduced-linear encode **named five triggers**, each with the failure class it prevents ([self-hosting.md](../design/self-hosting.md) → The named dialect-extension trigger; [decisions-pending.md](decisions-pending.md)): **(1)** a mechanizable **test-first-ordering / assertion gate** (the headline — prose → structure; the M14 `A==A`-oracle lint rides here as a candidate sub-check), **(2)** a structural **human-gate / checkpoint step kind** (the most-reused — planning's Settle, increment's halts), **(3)** a **portable gate-command knob**, **(4)** a **bounded fix-loop primitive + halt→re-run resumption**, **(5)** an **owner-assigned-artifact gate step** (the M8 genuine-spawn class). Conditional steps stay **barred** (the task-independence invariant). **Proves:** jigc can compose its *own* multi-phase, human-gated, fix-looping development workflows — the deepest self-hosting proof, beyond M12's reduced-linear slice. **Likely slices at planning** — the trigger fires on "the *second* harness workflow (increment or completion)," so the increment-workflow encode is the natural first slice; the full set may span more than one milestone. **Prereq #1 — built ahead of the dogfood on the human's direction.** VISION: closes the *exploratory outcome* M12 predicted.

### M16 · close the self-hosting loop — encode planning/completion + graduate the working-docs they drive — ✅ shipped (2026-06-09)
**Re-scoped 2026-06-09 (the fuse decision, [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md)):** M15 sliced to the increment-workflow only, so M16's doctype drivers — the **planning and completion workflows** — are still unencoded. By earn-from-driver a doctype earns its schema from the workflow that authors it, so M16 now **encodes each driver and graduates its doctype in one motion** (the M13 `arch-doc` pattern), rather than retrofitting doctypes onto separately-encoded workflows. Two halves: **(1)** encode the **planning** workflow (Settle = the human-gate = the **checkpoint step kind M15 already built**, so *no new dialect primitive*) + graduate the **roadmap** + **deferral-ledger** ([decisions-pending.md](decisions-pending.md)) doctypes it authors; **(2)** encode the **completion** workflow — which forces the deferred **#5 owner-assigned-artifact gate** (the genuine-spawn class, a *new* dialect primitive) — + graduate the **decisions-log** ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md)) / completion-record doctype. Each doctype is an **engine/pack-split call** (a roadmap/ledger is broadly useful → dev pack; a decisions-log may be methodology-specific). The honest single-agent-spine bound holds as in M15 (the multi-agent orchestration stays the build harness's). **Likely slices at planning — planning-half first (cheaper, rides the checkpoint), completion-half second (builds #5).** **Proves:** jigc composes its full planning/completion workflows *and* manages its own methodology's documents — the self-hosting loop fully closed. **Prereq #2 of the adoption arc.** Was M13 half-#2 + the M15-deferred planning/completion encode, now fused.

### M17 · dogfood + measurement
**Planned + settled 2026-06-12** ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → M17 planning; design home [measurement.md](../design/measurement.md)). Run jigc to develop **real** projects, and **measure whether it helps the agent** — the three-way cut applied to measurement itself: **mechanized facts** — **adapter-adherence** (managed-doc writes *through* jigc vs. OOB edits, deduped per path per run — the core-bet signal, since the adapter is *enforced-not-sandboxed*) and **drift-caught** (finalize blocks keyed on a distinct validation-blocked exit code — the *validate-against-reality* payoff) — · **protocol-counted facts** — **build-health** (halts / fix-rounds / audit-findings, counted from transcript + hook log per written rule, *not* engine-emitted; this homes the M12-deferred quantitative comparison **as protocol**, its hand-run-trend comparator **superseded by the A/B control**) — · the **verdict** ("is the agent working well with jigc?") a **human/LLM-judge slot**, never an engine opinion. Capture = a versioned PostToolUse **hook artifact** + three binary soundness increments (finalize-success findings emission · absorb baseline-advance at landed finalize · the exit code); the per-run **`dogfood-record`** doctype (spiked green through the binary at planning) is authored through jigc in the twin and exported. **Three real test cases, pilot-first behind a four-condition gate:** (1) the **pilot** — `gherrink-galey` (unlike-jigc, TS — jigc-shaped gates can't flatter a jigc-shaped target, the M12 lesson) **+ the A/B control** (twin /tmp copies at one baseline, matched real intent as a single dev-task vs. the repo's own static `CLAUDE.md`); (2) **`gherrink-lacon`** — existing project with a real docs corpus composed in (exercises M9's ingest/detect-and-route — *note:* non-conformant foreign docs route to a human, not auto-migrate, capping how "automatic" this case feels — [legacy auto-migration](decisions-pending.md) stays deferred; the one case where doc↔code genuinely adjudicates, Rust); (3) a **human-driven greenfield** from `jigc setup` onward (intent named at the pilot gate). Every measured run plants the **seeded-failure obligation** (one OOB edit + one bad finalize, each registering exactly once) against the green-by-construction FAIL mode. A six-item **pre-fix set** (run-breakers verified at planning) lands before the pilot; all other **gaps fill need-driven** — whatever the dogfoods surface as actually-blocking is fixed *because it hurt*. **Proves:** the founding thesis — jigc makes a coding agent measurably more correct/effective than a static `CLAUDE.md` — on real projects, not a controlled spike. **M17 closes the roadmap spine.** *(Outcome, 2026-06-13: the thesis was **not** demonstrated as a universal claim — two controlled comparisons tied / favored static at moderate task size, and the maintenance-over-time advantage turned out untested. Value is scope-bound; see [completions/artifacts/M17/VERDICT.md](../completions/artifacts/M17/VERDICT.md) + the native-grain test. M18 below pursues the strongest remaining shot.)*

## M18 · doc↔code maintenance over time — close the gap, then test the thesis where it should win

**Created 2026-06-13**, post-spine, from the native-grain-test scoping finding ([completions/artifacts/M17/native-grain-test/](../completions/artifacts/M17/native-grain-test/); [decisions-pending.md](decisions-pending.md)). Three probes established that **jigc enforces doc↔code integrity at *authoring* time, not over time**: a committed doc's `implemented-by` anchor that the code renames out from under it is **not** caught at an unrelated finalize, **not** caught on doc-drift/absorb, and caught **only** on deliberate re-author — and **no store-wide `jigc validate`** exists. So "living documentation that stays honest against the code" (VISION's strongest distinctive claim) is **not built as a passive/over-time guarantee**. This milestone closes that gap and then tests the claim where jigc *should* beat a static file — over time, not one-shot. Two halves: **(1) build** a store-wide on-demand doc↔code re-validation surface (a `jigc validate`/`check` that sweeps every committed doc's code-anchors against current code and reports stale anchors — the passive detector the claim needs; design the command + its severity + its relation to the task-scoped gate at milestone planning); **(2) test** the maintenance/drift-over-time thesis — jigc-with-store-validate vs. a static doc set over a pre-registered code-evolution sequence (renames, deletions, symbol moves), with an independent judge. **Catch-count alone is tautological** (the static arm has no detector, so it rots 100% by construction); the pre-registration must instead measure a **refutable net value** — true stale-anchor catches **minus** false-positive friction on legitimate refactors **minus** repair-loop cost — framed to prove-or-refute. **Honest bound carried in:** `symbol-exists` catches **structural** anchor drift, never **behavioral** drift (the prose lies while the symbol still exists) — name it, don't paper over it. **Proves (or refutes):** whether jigc's distinctive over-time advantage is real once the detector exists — the founding thesis's best remaining shot, the one-shot comparisons having tied. Milestone-level only; decomposed by the [milestone-planning workflow](milestone-planning-workflow.md) at pickup.

*(Outcome, 2026-06-13. **Build half: shipped + audited clean** — top-level `jigc validate` (read-only store-wide doc↔code sweep), 3 increments, milestone-completion audit found only 2 LOW findings (both fixed). **Test half: H0 — tie** ([completions/artifacts/M18/maintenance-test/](../completions/artifacts/M18/maintenance-test/)): 4 blind arms (2 jigc / 2 static) over a 5-step code-evolution sequence ended with **0 stale citations each**, every break repaired in-step, no over-editing. The over-time advantage is **undemonstrated** — but under the **pre-registered session-compression bound** (one focused session with a telegraphing task list is the static arm's best case; `jigc validate` never fired because diligence never failed), so the tie **cannot refute** the over-time claim, and a win would have been strong. Restates the native-grain lesson at maintenance grain. Both independent judges surfaced one observational signal: both jigc arms let a component's **prose** degrade while its symbol stayed valid (`validate` green) — the **behavioral blind spot observed in the wild**. The owed regime: genuine cross-session separation, where diligence actually fails and the backstop earns its keep.)*

## Milestone 18 — store-wide doc↔code re-validation: decomposition

**Planned + settled 2026-06-13** ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → M18 planning; design home [validation.md](../design/validation.md) → Store-scope re-validation). The **build half** of M18: a new top-level **`jigc validate`** — a store-wide, on-demand, **read-only** doc↔code re-validation that sweeps every committed doc's `code-anchor` fields against current code and **reports** stale anchors (never gates a transaction, never auto-rewrites). Three increments, risk-first: the net-new committed-store enumeration first (the one thing that doesn't exist), then the probe wiring over it, then the command surface + exit policy. **No pack changes** — pure read-path over the three already-shipped code-anchor doctypes (`adr.cites-code`, `arch-doc.components/implemented-by`, `spec.criteria/maps-to-test`). The baseline was **verified by exercising the binary** at planning (the probe/snapshot/ingest spine is task-decoupled; a live spike drove the real `doc-code` probe over a committed store) — so this is reuse-below / net-new-at-the-enumeration, not a new spine. Built against [validation.md](../design/validation.md) → Store-scope re-validation; survived independent design review (one BLOCKING + two SHOULD-FIX baked back — the two-class exit rule, probe pre-flight, and the refutable test framing).

**The binary changes** (engine + CLI), so it must be **rebuilt + re-pinned between the build half and the test half** (a mid-test binary change contaminates the measured run).

## Increment 1 — Read-only committed-store anchor enumeration

**Deliverable:** a new engine function `enumerate_committed_surface(repo_root, schemas) -> Vec<TargetAnchor>` that walks every active schema `location:` (`<repo-root>/<location>/*.md`, deterministic + address-sorted), parses each committed doc, and collects its `code-anchor` leaves — **read-only**, mutating no `FileStateRecord` and no edge index. Exercised via fixtures, not yet wired to a probe or command.

**Grouped scope:**
- Lift the doc→anchor projection `collect_from_source` (+ helpers `is_code_anchor` / `scalar` / `resolve_check_id`) from private to `pub` so the store walk reuses it **verbatim** — the repeatable-section per-item walk is already proven, do not re-implement it ([validation.md](../design/validation.md) → Store-scope re-validation, "Mechanism").
- The new store walk is its **own** read-only `<location>/*.md` glob (reusing the proven `committed_store`/`load_committed` idiom for sorted enumeration); it **must not** route through `reconcile_committed_store` — that path mutates record+index via *absorb* and would re-baseline genuine drift when run task-less.
- **The multi-valued non-silent guard** (review B-bound / settled fork 3): replace the current silent `scalar()` drop of a `Value::List` code-anchor with a **loud guard finding**, so a future `0..*` code-anchor can never pass unchecked-but-green. No list-element enumeration is built (no shipped schema needs it).
- One doctype per `location:` is assumed (true for the three today); note it, don't build disambiguation.

**Proves:** the net-new enumeration produces the correct, address-sorted anchor set over a committed store (valid + dangling + list-valued fixtures), surfaces unrelated committed docs the task-scope guard deliberately excludes, fires the guard on a list value, and mutates nothing. Retires M18's only real risk (the walk that doesn't exist + the not-through-reconcile constraint).

## Increment 2 — Store-sweep engine entry point (snapshot → probe → report)

**Deliverable:** an engine entry point that takes the increment-1 anchor set, materializes the serializable `EffectiveStateSnapshot` (`working_tree_root = repo_root`) to a scratch path, drives the existing `doc-code` subprocess invoker (passed in, CLI-owned — the engine stays shell-free), and ingests the probe's findings + the `pack-probe-integrity.*` meta-findings into a `ValidationReport`. Exercised by driving the **real** `doc-code` probe over a committed-store fixture.

**Grouped scope:**
- Reuse the M10 spine wholesale — `ProbeRequest` / `EffectiveStateSnapshot::new` / `ingest_probe_run` are target-agnostic (spike-confirmed); no probe-seam Ctx extension.
- The snapshot scratch path is a temp dir, **never** under a managed `location:`.
- Findings carry the same `(probe, check)` severity assignment as the task gate (the M6 post-pass) — `doc-code.symbol-exists` / `criterion-maps-to-test` blocking-by-default content findings; the probe-integrity meta-findings intrinsic-blocking and untunable.

**Proves:** the real probe runs over committed anchors — a dangling/renamed/deleted symbol → blocking `doc-code` content finding, a valid one → clean; a probe timeout/crash/malformed-output → a `pack-probe-integrity.*` meta-finding in the report (the input to increment 3's exit rule). Builds on increment 1.

## Increment 3 — The `jigc validate` command (surface, pre-flight, two-class exit)

**Deliverable:** a new top-level **`jigc validate`** that runs the store sweep end-to-end and renders the report — the user-facing deliverable and the milestone's acceptance flow. The realization of VISION's named `jigc validate [target]`, scoped to doc↔code; the four-target breadth stays a named-but-unbuilt envelope.

**Grouped scope:**
- Add the `Validate` variant to the top-level clap `Command` enum + a `run_validate_store` handler (mirroring `run_ingest`/`run_upgrade`): discover repo root, call the increment-2 entry point passing the CLI `doc-code` invoker, render via the existing `render::validation` (agent-text + json).
- **Probe pre-flight (review S2):** if the `doc-code` binary is not resolvable beside `jigc` (or via `JIGC_DOC_CODE_PROBE`), exit non-zero with **one** operational error ("`doc-code` probe not found"), not N per-anchor crash meta-findings.
- **Two-class exit rule (review B1):** content-only run (stale-anchor findings, or none) → **exit 0** (detect-and-report; the `ingest` precedent); **any** `pack-probe-integrity.*` meta-finding present → **non-zero exit** (the probe didn't run; the sweep can't claim a trustworthy result). This is *not* a transaction gate — it is the command honestly reporting it could not complete.

**Proves the acceptance flow:** seed a committed store (an `arch-doc` + `adr` + `spec` with valid anchors) → rename a cited symbol in the code → `jigc validate` surfaces the now-stale anchor that an unrelated `task validate`/`finalize` would *not* catch (the over-time gap M18 closes), findings listed, **exit 0**; probe-absent → one operational error, non-zero; an injected probe failure → non-zero (never a falsely-green run). Built against [validation.md](../design/validation.md) → Store-scope re-validation.

*(Outcome, 2026-06-13. **Build half: shipped + audited clean** — the warn-only `pre-commit` backstop + the finalize success-relay, 2 increments, only 3 LOW audit findings (all fixed). **Test half: H0 — tie** ([completions/artifacts/M19/cross-session-test/](../completions/artifacts/M19/cross-session-test/)): 6 chains (3 jigc / 3 static) × 8 **cold-per-step** agents (genuine forgetting) over a no-cue sequence ended **0 stale citations, all six**. The comparative over-time advantage is **undemonstrated again** — but for a sharper reason: the **static arm was clean at every commit** (right-first-try diligence), while the **jigc arm was the only one that drifted** (transient stale citations up to 4-commit windows) and recovered via later **detector-prompted** cleanup — the J repair commits credit `jigc validate` by name. So unlike M18 the **backstop demonstrably fired + drove repair** (a real loop-closure capability gain), **but on drift that arose in jigc's own arm**, and it was neither a comparative end-state nor timeline win. Honest mechanism: net-dependent cleanup latency, not moral hazard. Load-bearing bound: **warn-only/advisory** — the loop closed because agents heeded the warning, not as a guarantee. The regime where jigc would strictly win — first-try diligence leaving *residual* drift — wasn't reached; static didn't fail. Verdict Codex-passed.)*

**Test half (post-build, pre-registered — not an increment).** After the three increments land and the binary is rebuilt + re-pinned: a pre-registered maintenance/drift-over-time comparison (jigc-with-`validate` vs. a static doc set over a code-evolution sequence), plain markdown under `completions/artifacts/M18/`, independent Codex judge — the [M17 native-grain-test](../completions/artifacts/M17/native-grain-test/) template. **Catch-count alone is tautological** (the static arm has no detector); the pre-registration measures a **refutable net value** (true catches − false-positive friction − repair cost). **Honest bounds carried in:** `symbol-exists` catches **structural** anchor drift only, never **behavioral** prose drift; non-`.rs` `#symbol` silently passes (file-existence only).

## M19 · auto-firing doc↔code backstop + the cross-session drift test — make the detector fire, then test where it earns its keep

**Created 2026-06-13**, from the M18 test-half finding ([completions/artifacts/M18/maintenance-test/results.md](../completions/artifacts/M18/maintenance-test/results.md)). M18 built the store-wide `jigc validate` detector but it stayed **on-demand** and **never fired** in the test — a capable single session kept docs honest by diligence, so the backstop was never the thing that caught drift, and the over-time advantage came back **H0 (undemonstrated, not refuted)** under the session-compression bound. The owed regime: **genuine cross-session separation, where diligence actually fails and the backstop earns its keep** — but a backstop only earns its keep if it **fires without anyone remembering to run it** (an agent told to "run `jigc validate` each session" is just diligence one level up). So M19 has two halves: **(1) build** the **auto-firing integration** — a `jigc setup`-installed mechanism that runs `jigc validate` at a natural trigger with no agent action (candidate mechanisms, a planning fork: a **git pre-commit/pre-push hook** [assistant-neutral, fires on VCS events]; the **Claude Code `SessionStart` adapter hook** surfacing committed-store drift [rides the seam the profile already declares — [assistant-adapter.md](../design/assistant-adapter.md)]; and/or an emitted **CI recipe**); with the **report-only-warn vs blocking** severity a fork the M18 report-only decision + the masking-trap concern bear on (a *blocking* pre-commit store-sweep would reject commits for unrelated pre-existing drift — exactly what M18 avoided). **(2) test** the maintenance/drift thesis under **genuine cross-session forgetting** — a fresh, cold, blind subagent *per step* that does not know prior steps touched the docs (the real forgetting M18's compression suppressed), jigc-arm-with-auto-fire-backstop vs static-arm-with-none, measuring whether the backstop **fires and drives repair** (the thing that didn't happen in M18), the per-step accumulate-vs-caught timeline, and the final stale-citation count, with independent judges. **Honest bounds carried in:** structural anchor drift only (`symbol-exists`), never behavioral prose drift (M18 observed jigc-green-over-drifted-prose in the wild); and the **biggest risk — the test must stay refutable** (not foregone): cold agents fix their *own* step's break by diligence, so drift only accumulates when a break *slips* + the backstop catches prior-accumulated drift; making slips happen without rigging is the milestone's hardest design problem, owned by the test pre-registration. **Proves (or refutes):** whether jigc's distinctive over-time advantage is real once the detector fires automatically and forgetting is genuine — the M18-owed regime. **Build contract vs test hypothesis, kept distinct (M19 review S2):** the build *contract* is that the backstop **surfaces** store drift automatically — it is **warn-only and does NOT force repair** (it can't, by the report-only/no-auto-author invariants, and the agent can ignore a warning). The test *hypothesis* is whether that automatic surfacing **changes a cold agent's repair behavior** vs no detector. These are different claims: firing-without-forcing may still reproduce M18's null if the cold agent ignores the relayed warning, so **a tie remains a possible, honest outcome — not a refutation** — and the pre-registration must keep tie/static-win reachable, score *net value* (catches − false-positive friction − repair cost, the M18 framing), and pre-state what observable in the warning is expected to drive repair. **No-cue slips are fair, and the pre-reg must say why (review S3):** "code changed; the doc that cites it wasn't in this session's scope" *is* the realistic over-time drift case — but the design must justify it as fair (not static-arm-rigging) and **score the repair, not just the catch** (a surfaced-but-unrepaired finding scores as no-win). Design home: [assistant-adapter.md](../design/assistant-adapter.md) (the hook/install seam) + [validation.md](../design/validation.md) (the store-sweep it fires) + [finalize.md](../design/finalize.md) (the success-relay). Build half decomposes into increments; test half is a post-build pre-registered experiment (not an increment). Milestone-level only; decomposed by the [milestone-planning workflow](milestone-planning-workflow.md) at pickup.

## Milestone 19 — auto-firing doc↔code backstop: decomposition

**Planned + settled 2026-06-13** ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → M19 planning; design homes [assistant-adapter.md](../design/assistant-adapter.md) → neutral install, [finalize.md](../design/finalize.md) → 6. Commit, [validation.md](../design/validation.md) → Auto-firing the sweep). The **build half** of M19: make the M18 store-wide `jigc validate` detector **fire automatically, warn-only, with no agent action**, and ensure its warning **reaches an agent committing via `jigc finalize`**. Two increments, risk-first: the net-new git-hook install (the mechanism + its load-bearing output discipline) first, then the small finalize relay that closes the loop. **No pack changes, no new doctype** — pure integration over the shipped `jigc validate`. Baseline verified by exercising the binary at planning (setup's data-driven hook install + non-destructive merge; `jigc validate` report-only behavior; the finalize-swallows-hook-output spike); survived independent design review (2 BLOCKING + 3 SHOULD-FIX baked back). **The binary changes** (CLI/setup + finalize), so it must be **rebuilt + re-pinned between the build half and the test half**.

## Increment 1 — The auto-firing git `pre-commit` hook (install + output discipline)

**Deliverable:** `jigc setup` installs an assistant-neutral `.git/hooks/pre-commit` that runs `jigc validate` and **warns the committer only on real doc↔code drift**, always exiting 0 (warn-only — never blocks). Exercised through the real binary + real `git commit`s.

**Grouped scope:**
- A new **assistant-neutral install step** in `setup::install` (distinct from the per-assistant adapter profile — [assistant-adapter.md](../design/assistant-adapter.md) → neutral install responsibilities), writing a **sentinel-marked, idempotent, non-destructive** `pre-commit` hook into the repo's **real** hooks dir (honor `core.hooksPath`; handle the git-worktree `.git`-is-a-file case; preserve/wrap any existing `pre-commit`); regenerated on every `setup`.
- **The hook's output discipline (review B2 — the load-bearing contract):** the hook runs `jigc validate --format json`, surfaces a human-readable warning **only when the report carries `doc-code.*` content findings**, and **prints nothing + exits 0** on (i) clean, (ii) not-a-jigc-project, (iii) probe-missing, (iv) any `pack-probe-integrity` meta-finding, and (v) `jigc` absent. It **keys on findings, not the exit code** (the exit code is wrong-way-round). The PATH-vs-absolute-`jigc` choice is decided + implemented (the stale-binary hazard).
- The hook is an **install artifact** (peer to `.claude/settings.json`) — outside the managed-doc store, `file-state`, and reconciliation (review N2); `jigc setup` (the deterministic CLI) writes it, no determinism-boundary tension.

**Proves:** with the hook installed, a committed-store **stale anchor surfaces as a warning at `git commit` time and the commit still succeeds** (warn-only); a **clean store / unconfigured repo / missing probe commits silently** (no noise, never broken); the install is **idempotent + non-destructive** (a pre-existing `pre-commit` is preserved) and **honors `core.hooksPath` + worktrees**. The backstop fires automatically with no agent action — the thing M18 lacked. Built against [assistant-adapter.md](../design/assistant-adapter.md) + [validation.md](../design/validation.md).

## Increment 2 — `finalize` relays hook output on success (the loop-closer)

**Deliverable:** `jigc finalize`'s `git_commit` relays captured hook stdout/stderr to the agent on a **successful** commit (today only on failure), so the warn-only backstop's warning reaches an agent committing through finalize — closing the loop the spike exposed.

**Grouped scope:**
- `git_commit` ([task.rs](../crates/cli/src/task.rs)) surfaces captured stdout/stderr on **success** in a **clearly delimited section after** the success result — **never** inside the routing footer or the `--format json` envelope (review S1). The (general — one combined git stream, any non-blocking hook) behavior change is documented.
- **Aggregate-commit only (review B1):** the per-sub-task `commit_empty_message` (tree-empty `squash: false` fan-out commits) does **not** relay; only the aggregate `git_commit` does.
- Resolves [finalize.md](../design/finalize.md)'s "commit-msg hook output capture" open question (the success-relay specifically).

**Proves:** a non-blocking `pre-commit` hook's success output **reaches the agent through `jigc task finalize`** (where today it is swallowed), lands in a delimited section without corrupting the footer/JSON envelope, and a `squash: false` fan-out finalize relays only the aggregate commit. The detect→surface loop closes for an agent committing via jigc. Built against [finalize.md](../design/finalize.md) → 6. Commit.

**Test half (post-build, pre-registered — not an increment).** After both increments land and the binary is rebuilt + re-pinned: the maintenance/drift test re-run under **genuine cross-session forgetting** — a fresh **cold blind subagent per step** (blind to prior steps having touched docs), the jigc arm with the **warn-only backstop installed** vs a static arm with none, over a **no-cue induced-slip** sequence (each step breaks a citation in a doc its task list doesn't mention). Plain markdown under `completions/artifacts/M19/`, independent judges — the [M18 maintenance-test](../completions/artifacts/M18/maintenance-test/) template. **Carry in:** the build-contract-vs-test-hypothesis split (the backstop *surfaces*, warn-only, never *forces* repair — a tie stays an honest outcome), **net-value** scoring (catches − false-positive friction − repair cost), the repair scored (not just the catch), and the honest bounds (structural drift only; behavioral prose drift out of frame).

## M20 · hardening for real-world install — `cargo install` + the 3-target validate envelope — ✅ shipped (2026-06-14)

**Created 2026-06-14**, the first milestone under **option B** — accept the founding thesis is scope-bound, harden/ship what's *built* ([VISION.md](../VISION.md) → What is proven; [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-06-14 direction + M20 planning). The roadmap spine closed at M17; M20 is **post-spine hardening**, not a new differentiator — it makes the built surface *shippable and trustworthy to an outside user*. Scoped from the M19 handover's three deferred adoption gaps; the gap probe (4 `gap-detector`s over a verified baseline) re-scoped it to **two increments** — **multi-assistant adapter profiles split out to its own future milestone** (reach, not hardening; needs an assistant named; risks reopening the M8 launch-template rule — [decisions-pending.md](decisions-pending.md)). **Proves:** a fresh `cargo install jigc` yields a working `jigc setup` + `jigc validate` (no 418MB binary, no missing-probe misfire), and store-wide `jigc validate` covers the full three-target integrity envelope (doc↔code + workflow↔refs + read-only file↔CLI-state). **No new doctype**; the validate work adds **no new check ids**. Design homes: [module-layout.md](module-layout.md) → Probe distribution; [validation.md](../design/validation.md) → Completing the envelope. Milestone-level decomposition below.

## Milestone 20 — hardening (cargo install + validate envelope): decomposition

**Planned + settled 2026-06-14** ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → M20 planning). Baseline verified by three `capability-auditor`s exercising the binary; survived independent design review (2 BLOCKING + 4 SHOULD-FIX baked before this cut). Two increments, **risk-first**: the build-system surgery (probe distribution) first — the actual shipping blocker and the highest-uncertainty change — then the engine-native envelope completion. Neither depends on the other (the envelope checks are in-process, not the probe). **The binary changes** (build/embed + CLI), so it must be **rebuilt + re-pinned** before any exercise that asserts install behavior.

## Increment 1 — Probe rides in the binary: `cargo install` becomes a supported channel

**Deliverable:** a `cargo install`-style (non-build-tree) install of `jigc` produces a reasonably-sized binary whose `jigc setup` places the `doc-code` probe beside it, so `jigc validate` / code-anchor finalize work on a fresh machine with no manual probe copy. Exercised against a real built binary, no `JIGC_DOC_CODE_PROBE` override.

**Grouped scope:**
- **De-bloat the embed (the real site):** `pack.rs`'s `include_dir!("$CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR/pack")` sweeps the probe's gitignored ~401MB `target/` because the probe source sits under `pack/probes/`. Relocate the probe source out of the embedded `pack/` tree (or scope the embed to exclude `probes/`) so `PACK` carries only real pack content (~55KB); **verify embedded pack content — schemas/workflows/steps/config — still loads unchanged.**
- **Embed + extract the probe (net-new — it isn't embedded today, only build-script-copied):** `include_bytes!` the built ~2MB `doc-code` binary (via an `OUT_DIR` indirection `build.rs` populates — confirm build-ordering at pickup, an (I)-pick); a **neutral `jigc setup` install step** writes it to `current_exe().parent()` with the exec bit, **write-if-absent-or-byte-different** (don't clobber a build-tree sibling), **one operational error** on a read-only target. First machine-global setup write (peer to, but not repo-local like, the M19 hook).
- **Absence-vs-crash fix:** port M18's store-scope `require_doc_code_probe` pre-flight to the **task** path — probe-absent → one operational error, not a floored per-anchor `pack-probe-integrity.crash`.
- Update **QUICKSTART.md** (install no longer needs the manual `doc-code` copy; note the setup-extract + the M19 hook the current text omits).

**Proves:** `cargo install` → `jigc setup` → `jigc validate` works on a fresh machine; the binary is ~MBs, not ~400MB; a missing probe reports once, cleanly, at **both** store and task scope; embedded pack content is intact. Built against [module-layout.md](module-layout.md) → Probe distribution.

## Increment 2 — Complete the `jigc validate` envelope: workflow↔refs + read-only file↔CLI-state

**Deliverable:** store-wide `jigc validate` reports, alongside doc↔code, (a) workflow-definition referential drift and (b) committed-doc content drift — read-only, never gating. Exercised through the real binary over a store carrying a seeded dangling workflow-ref and an out-of-band file edit.

**Grouped scope:**
- **workflow↔refs store target:** deterministic, address-sorted enumeration of every cascade-resolved workflow definition; per-def run the **task-independent** checks (`include-resolves`, `include-cycle-absent`, `body-include-only`, the three `*-marker-not-shadowed` checks, `fan-out-join-paired`) + a **catalog-membership-only** command-ref path (`catalog.get(id)`, **no** `render_command` — the design-review B1 fix that avoids fabricated `task.*` drift). The task-data checks (`placeholder-resolves`, `at-marker-on-non-scalar`) stay at `jigc start`. Net-new: the enumeration + per-def driver + the membership-only path.
- **read-only file↔CLI-state twin:** compare each committed managed doc's on-disk hash to its `FileStateRecord`, **no record write**, **not** via `reconcile_committed_store`; honor the three pinned edges (UNKNOWN → un-baselined informational, not silent-clean / not exit-flipping; a **store-scope route**, not `reconcile`; **content-drift-on-present-docs only** — rename/missing stays task-scope).
- **Host three families under the uniform exit rule:** reshape `validate_store` from its doc-code-single-probe shape to carry three families; content findings exit 0, only `pack-probe-integrity`/probe-missing exits non-zero.
- Reuse `workflow-refs.*` + `file-state.hash-matches` **check ids** (no new ids/knobs); the M19 pre-commit hook stays doc↔code-keyed.

**Proves:** `jigc validate` surfaces a dangling workflow-ref and a file-state drift as report-only content findings (exit 0) beside doc↔code staleness; the file-state twin does **not** re-baseline the drift it reports (mutation-free, verified); the workflow↔refs target emits **no** false `task-ref-in-no-task-workflow` drift over the real dev pack. Built against [validation.md](../design/validation.md) → Completing the envelope.

**Outcome (2026-06-14).** Both increments built + **independently validated clean, 0 fix rounds**; milestone-completion audit (code-review + real-`cargo install` e2e) passed — verified end-to-end through an installed 6.4MB binary (probe extracts at setup + fires; one operational error on absence at both scopes; three families fold under the uniform exit rule; file-state twin byte-for-byte mutation-free; no false drift over the dev pack; order-invariant). 4 LOW findings, all **fixed** (not deferred): the probe-extract write policy → **heal/upgrade** (overwrite iff bytes differ — fixes upgrade-staleness; human-gated at completion); store-sweep schemas → **cascade-aware** (`all_schemas`, honoring project shadows); two doc/test-precision fixes. Commits `290c8f9…b91a7de` (14). [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → M20 completion.

## M21 · production-readiness for real-project live testing — embed methodology + Flow-A cold-start + full Flow-B (minus auto-migration) — ✅ shipped (2026-06-14)

**Created 2026-06-14**, the second post-spine hardening milestone ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-06-14 direction pivot + M21 planning). Closes the blockers so the human's **two manual live tests** run on a clean install: **Flow A** (new project — vision→milestones→MVP) and **Flow B** (existing project — track docs, then clean up). Baseline re-verified by 4 `capability-auditor`s + 4 `gap-detector`s exercising the real binary (HEAD `71c3df6`); the central forks were settled and **a precedence-order spike** found the clean path; survived an independent design review (**2 BLOCKING + 3 SHOULD + 2 CONSIDER, all baked** before this cut). **Proves:** a fresh `cargo install jigc` → `jigc setup` gives a real project the **methodology pack embedded + auto-wired** (compose `[dev ▸ methodology]`, dev-highest → `default-workflow=router`, `commit` keeps `implements→spec`, full milestone/roadmap authoring out of the box); the Flow-A **zero-commit cold-start crash is gone** and the milestone/ingest verbs are **discoverable**; and **full Flow-B** (recursive scan + the G4 baseline-adopt safety gate + un-manage/uninstall teardown + discoverability) lands — **minus** auto-migration (G1) + new doctype coverage (G2 `changelog`/…), deferred **paired** to a dedicated post-M21 milestone (a `changelog` doctype is unusable for an existing project without auto-migration). **No new doctype, no new check ids, no engine knob-merge primitive** (the collisions resolve by precedence order alone). Design homes: [multi-pack.md](../design/multi-pack.md) → Embedded second pack + setup auto-wiring; [module-layout.md](module-layout.md) → Pack distribution; [project-setup.md](../design/project-setup.md) → Flow 2 hardening. Milestone-level decomposition below.

## Milestone 21 — production-readiness (embed methodology + cold-start + full Flow-B): decomposition

**Planned + settled 2026-06-14** ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → M21 planning). **Four increments, risk-first + linear:** the embed+compose surgery first (the highest-uncertainty change and the Flow-A milestone wall), then the Flow-A cold-start completers, then the two Flow-B increments (safety+reach, then teardown). **The binary changes** (a second `include_dir!` + factory + `setup` + new verbs), so it must be **rebuilt + re-pinned** (`cargo install --path crates/cli` + `jigc setup`-extract, the M20 re-pin flow) before any exercise that asserts install/compose behavior. Cold-start friction *not* re-fixed: `set-slot --from-file -` stdin was a **stale** claim (already works); the commit `scope`/`body` "optional" friction stays the existing optional-slot deferral (pack-data/UX, no engine primitive).

## Increment 1 — Embed the methodology pack + compose it at setup (the Flow-A milestone wall)

**Deliverable:** a clean `jigc setup` that has written the composition marker gives a real project the **dev + methodology** surface in-binary — `jigc doc create roadmap` resolves, the methodology workflows compose, `default-workflow` resolves to `router`, the composed `commit` keeps `implements→spec` — with **no** on-disk pack tree and **no** `packs.yaml` path. Absent the marker, the pack-set is `[dev]`, byte-identical to today. Exercised against a real rebuilt binary.

**Grouped scope:**
- **Field-carrying `EmbeddedPack`** — parameterize the unit struct over a selected `&'static Dir` (`crates/cli/src/pack.rs:50,53`) so two embedded packs can be composed; preserve the dev-only `pack_version = binary version` invariant for both.
- **Second `include_dir!`** of the ~45KB `packs/methodology/` tree (pure data — **verify no `target/`/build-tree is swept**, the M20 bloat lesson; confirm the embedded methodology content loads).
- **Factory composes `[dev ▸ methodology]` dev-highest** — a `make_pack`/`make_pack_from` path that builds `CompositePack([dev_embedded, methodology_embedded])` **when** `.jigc/config/packs.yaml` carries `compose-embedded-methodology: true` (the key the factory already reads pre-cascade — no new discovery walk). Marker + extra listed filesystem packs is **out of scope** (route/defer).
- **`jigc setup` writes the marker** — a neutral install step writing `compose-embedded-methodology: true` into `.jigc/config/packs.yaml`; idempotent, non-destructive.
- Add a **worked-example flow** for the compose-both-at-setup path (and note in [worked-examples.md](../design/worked-examples.md) flow 15 that `JIGC_PACK_DIR` stays the explicit/dogfood channel).

**Proves:** with the marker, `jigc start --explain` shows `default-workflow → won by dev` / `commit → won by dev`, `jigc doc create roadmap` + `jigc start --workflow planning` work, and methodology's `dev-task` body still composes its own test-first `implement` (pack-local); **without** the marker, the surface + composition bytes are byte-identical to the pre-M21 dev-only path (the single-pack floor); methodology-alone (`JIGC_PACK_DIR`) is unchanged. Built against [multi-pack.md](../design/multi-pack.md) → Embedded second pack; [module-layout.md](module-layout.md) → Pack distribution.

**Build note (2026-06-14) — two inserted tasks (M14 debt surfaced when composition first flows through the committed paths).** Writing the marker (T3) composes `[dev ▸ methodology]` through paths M14 never exercised with two packs, exposing two settled-design violations fixed as inserted tasks **before** T3: **(T2a)** `JIGC_PACK_DIR` (the explicit/dogfood channel) must **supersede** the marker — T2 had the marker override it, breaking methodology-alone; fix = an `explicit_base` guard in `make_pack_from_marker`. **(T2b)** the `jigc validate` store sweep resolved command-refs against **one flat catalog** (the precedence winner = dev's), not **pack-locally per workflow's origin pack** — violating [multi-pack.md](../design/multi-pack.md) → Pack-local body-reference resolution (`:79`); once methodology composes, its workflows' command-refs (`create-roadmap`/`create-log`/…) raise false `workflow-refs.command-ref-resolves` blocking findings. Fix = thread a per-workflow origin-pack catalog through `validate_store_families` + the check, mirroring `start.rs`'s `load_catalog(origin)` idiom (the compose/start path already complies). Neither is a new check id or a design fork — both are settled-design compliance the store sweep predated.

## Increment 2 — Flow-A cold-start: zero-commit survival + off-catalog discoverability

**Deliverable:** the literal first commands of a new project run on a **fresh `git init` (zero commits)**, and a bare `jigc start` orientation **names the off-catalog `planning` + `ingest-existing` verbs** so milestones (and the existing-project on-ramp) are discoverable without prior knowledge.

**Grouped scope:**
- **Zero-commit sentinel** — `read_head` (`start.rs:2255` + the milestone twin `milestone.rs:692`) treats an empty `HEAD` as an **empty-tree-sentinel** `BasePin` (canonical empty-tree SHA) rather than bailing, so every `creates-task` workflow (and the milestone mint) runs pre-first-commit; the first finalize diffs against the empty tree and commits cleanly.
- **Off-catalog discoverability (G6)** — orientation route-prose that names `planning` (`selectable:false`, off-router by the M16 invariant — **no flip**) and `ingest-existing` (`creates-task:false`), the same shape for each, so "milestones out of the box" is actually delivered (the router catalog alone never surfaces `planning` — design-review B1).

**Proves:** in a fresh zero-commit repo, `jigc setup` then `jigc start --workflow project-setup` (and `--workflow single-task`, and the milestone mint) run without the `git rev-parse HEAD` crash; bare `jigc start` orientation text names the `planning` and `ingest-existing` verbs. Built against [project-setup.md](../design/project-setup.md) → Flow 2 hardening; [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → M21 planning.

## Increment 3 — Flow-B safety + reach: the G4 baseline-adopt gate + recursive scan

**Deliverable:** `finalize`/`validate` no longer silently adopts an unvetted foreign doc sitting in a `location:` dir, and `jigc ingest` discovers docs in nested directories — so an existing project's doc landscape is seen and the trust hazard is closed.

**Grouped scope:**
- **G4 conformance gate** — `crates/engine/src/file_state.rs` `reconcile_committed`'s `UNKNOWN` arm (`:189-193`) runs `parse_sections` + `schema_conformance` (the call its sibling DRIFTED arm `:201-216` already makes) **before** recording; a non-conformant file is **routed as advisory and NOT recorded** (so it re-fires every finalize until the human resolves it — the route names the corrective verb), while a conformant fresh-checkout doc still baselines cleanly (preserves the M20 clean-store guarantee). Advisory, never blocking.
- **Recursive scan (G3)** — `crates/engine/src/ingest.rs` `discover_candidates` (`:49`) walks subdirectories (keeping the `.jigc/` exclusion + `BTreeSet` sort-determinism) so `wiki/`, nested `docs/sub/`, `rfcs/` are reachable.

**Proves:** a freeform `notes.md` placed in `decisions/` is routed (advisory) and **not** baseline-adopted at finalize, and the advisory recurs on a second finalize; a nested `docs/sub/x.md` appears in `jigc ingest` output; a conformant jigc-minted doc on a fresh checkout still baselines without a false advisory. Built against [project-setup.md](../design/project-setup.md) → Flow 2 hardening.

## Increment 4 — Flow-B teardown: un-manage a doc + repo-local `jigc uninstall`

**Deliverable:** two cleanup verbs that let a human reverse jigc's footprint — drop a single managed doc, or tear down the whole repo-local install — both idempotent and non-destructive to the user's file bytes.

**Grouped scope:**
- **Un-manage a doc** — a verb that drops one managed doc from the `FileStateRecord` + its `edges.json` entries (the inverse of `adopt`'s register-only mutation), **leaving the file bytes on disk**; idempotent (re-run on an already-unmanaged doc is a clean no-op).
- **`jigc uninstall` (repo-local only)** — remove `.jigc/`, unwire the `CLAUDE.md` import line + the `.claude/settings.json` allowlist entry + the `compose-embedded-methodology` marker; **explicitly NOT** the machine-global `doc-code` probe sibling (shared across repos — design-review B2); idempotent (second run is a clean no-op); removes exactly the enumerated repo-local setup-created set.

**Proves:** un-manage drops a doc from jigc's index/state with its on-disk bytes unchanged and is idempotent; `jigc uninstall` removes the repo-local footprint, leaves the probe sibling intact (a sibling repo's `jigc validate` still works), and a second `uninstall` is a clean no-op. Built against [project-setup.md](../design/project-setup.md) → Flow 2 hardening.

**Outcome (2026-06-14).** All four increments built; the build harness **halted once** mid-increment-1 (T3) on **M14 debt** — the `jigc validate` store sweep was never made pack-local for command-refs and `JIGC_PACK_DIR` precedence was inverted — both settled-design-compliance fixes landed as inserted tasks **T2a/T2b** before T3 (the halt prevented shipping a broken `validate` over composed packs). Increment 1 was finished + **independently validated clean** (single-pack floor byte-identical, no over-correction, no scope creep) before a `skipThrough:1` re-launch built increments 2–4. Milestone-completion audit: **code-review + e2e both passed** — all 10 e2e scenarios green through the freshly-built real binary (embed+compose dev-highest, marker, byte-identity floor, zero-commit cold-start + clean first finalize, G6 discoverability, G4 baseline-adopt gate on **both** the store-sweep and finalize-post-commit paths, recursive ingest scan, un-manage + repo-local uninstall with the machine-global probe intact). **1 LOW finding, fixed** (not deferred): `inject_reference` was lossy so the `CLAUDE.md` setup→uninstall round-trip wasn't byte-identical for a non-newline-terminated original — made `inject` reversible (`4facedf`). Commits `0567357…4facedf` (20). **No new doctype, no new check ids, no engine knob-merge primitive** — the collisions resolved by precedence order alone. [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → M21 completion.

## M22 · doctype-expansion — the `changelog` doctype + the four engine lifts it earns — ✅ shipped (2026-06-14)

**Created 2026-06-14**, the first of the two milestones the paired post-M21 deferral was **split** into ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-06-14 split; auto-migration G1 is the *separate later* milestone). Serves **Flow A** (a new project authors a managed doc *through* jigc). Baseline verified by 3 `capability-auditor`s + 4 `gap-detector`s exercising the real binary (one **spiked both schema shapes** — route-around byte-stable, multi-level a real engine change); survived an independent design review (**1 BLOCKING + 4 SHOULD + 5 CONSIDER, all baked** before this cut — the BLOCKING B1 resolved by dropping the release's leading prose slot for an optional `link` field). **Proves:** a new project authors a managed, validated **`changelog`** singleton through jigc — a real-project doc with a **nested** repeatable (release → change-group), cold-created then warm-appended byte-stable + finalize-gated (worked-examples flow 24). The human's call ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → doctype-expansion planning): **build the four latent engine capabilities, don't route around them** — each lands a real in-milestone target: **`Leaf::Repeatable`** (the release nesting), the **multi-word section-id** parser fix (`## Unreleased Changes`), the **optional slot/field** flag (an optional release `link`), and **doc-level `set: on-create` + `default:`** (honoring `adr`'s long-broken `date`/`status`). Driver = a standalone `record-change` workflow + a `single-task` `allows-create` fold-in (both dev-pack-local). **Honest bound:** Flow-A only — an existing `CHANGELOG.md` still routes `needs-reconcile` until auto-migration (G1). The rest of the project-doctype set is **mapped, not built** ([doctype-map.md](doctype-map.md) → candidate set). **The binary changes** (a new schema variant + parser/validator/writer recursion + a new doctype/workflow), so it must be **rebuilt + re-pinned** (`cargo install --path crates/cli` + `jigc setup`-extract) before any exercise. Design home: [design/changelog.md](../design/changelog.md). Decomposition below.

## Milestone 22 — doctype-expansion (`changelog` + four engine lifts): decomposition

**Planned + settled 2026-06-14** ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → doctype-expansion planning; design [changelog.md](../design/changelog.md)). **Five increments, risk-first + linear:** the LARGE `Leaf::Repeatable` lift first (fail-fast on the headline risk, and the changelog schema can't load without it), then the two small independent capability fixes, then the isolated adr-scoped doc-level-on-create fix (its front-matter-test churn quarantined — review finding S2), then the changelog doctype + driver that consumes the prior four (earn-from-driver). Every gate carries the forward-review **red obligation** (fire-and-block / round-trip on real input before green — the vacuously-green-managed-doc failure class) and spikes the **cold/empty** state. The `structural-grammar`/`document-type-schema` open-question flips land **at build** in the increment that ships each capability (a flip documents a *landed* capability).

## Increment 1 — `Leaf::Repeatable`: the multi-level repetition lift (the headline risk)

**Deliverable:** the schema model expresses a repeatable nested inside a repeatable, and a two-level instance parses, renders, validates, and is addressable — round-tripping byte-stable. General recursion with a documented depth cap; the changelog exercises exactly two levels.

**Grouped scope:**
- **Schema variant** — add `Leaf::Repeatable(Repeatable)` to `crates/engine/src/schema.rs` (`Leaf = Slot | Field` today); the loader parses a nested `repeatable:` under a block leaf. **Heading-depth grammar:** item heading level = `2 + nesting-depth` (section `##`, L1 item `###`, L2 item `####`), capped at **H6 (4 levels)** — a documented cap, not silent truncation.
- **Depth-aware recursive parse** — `parse.rs` (`ItemTemplate::from`, `parse_items`, `next_section_heading`, `is_reserved_depth`): the item-region scanner treats a deeper heading as a sub-item boundary within its parent's region and recurses per the schema block; the duplicate-anchor `seen` check **resets per parent item** (two releases may each carry `#added`; one release with two `#added` blocks).
- **Recursive render** — `write.rs` `render_item` emits headings at the schema depth and recurses; a nested item's `{#id}` anchor is deterministic from its `id-from`.
- **Recursive validate** — `validate.rs` `check_repeatable` recurses, running required-slot/required-field/field-value conformance over each nested item's leaves at every level.
- **Parent-scoped path locator (NOT a vocabulary extension — review finding S1)** — replace the global single-level `write::locate_item_block` (matches at `###`, by anchor alone, globally) with a locator that resolves `item/subitem` by walking each path segment within its parent's byte region; `set_item_slot`/`set_item_field`/`add_item` and the `target_surface.rs` address builder consume it, so `#section/item/subitem/leaf` resolves unambiguously.

**Proves:** a fixture doctype with a two-level repeatable round-trips byte-stable cold and warm; a hand-malformed nested entry (empty required nested slot) **blocks** at `schema-conformance.required-slot-present` naming the **nested** leaf address; two same-anchor groups within one parent block while across two parents pass; nested `add-item`/`set-slot`/`set-field` reach the nested item. Built against [changelog.md](../design/changelog.md) → engine work #1; flips the [structural-grammar.md](../design/structural-grammar.md) "one bounded level" open.

## Increment 2 — multi-word section-id round-trip (the parser fix)

**Deliverable:** a schema section id with a hyphen round-trips instead of making the whole doc non-reparseable.

**Grouped scope:**
- **`parse::heading_matches`** re-slugs the rendered heading before comparing (`slugify(text) == section_id`), symmetric with the title-casing writer; no writer change. Free-text item titles are unaffected (never matched against a section id).

**Proves:** a doctype with a `## Unreleased Changes` (`unreleased-changes`) section round-trips byte-stable; the pre-fix behaviour is pinned as the regression red. Built against [changelog.md](../design/changelog.md) → engine work #2; **narrows** the deferred `prd` repeatable-requirements blocker ([decisions-pending.md](decisions-pending.md)).

## Increment 3 — optional slot / field (the `optional:` flag)
